And though the atomist Democritus is one of the authors who speak of rhythm in the sense of form, it should be borne in mind that he does so under very precise conditions of fluctuation and that the forms made by atoms are primarily large, nonmetric aggregates, smooth spaces such as the air, the sea, or even the earth (magnae res). There is indeed such a thing as measured, cadenced rhythm, relating to the coursing of a river between its banks or to the form of a striated space; but there is also a rhythm without measure, which relates to the upswell of a flow, in other words, to the manner in which a fluid occupies a smooth space. This opposition, or rather this tension-limit between the two kinds of science - nomad, war machine science and royal, State science - reappears at different moments, on different levels. The work of Anne Querrien enables us to identify two of these moments; one is the construction of Gothic cathedrals | It is at sea, as Virilio shows, that the problem of the ***fleet in being*** <span style="color:green">[English in the French – naval doctrine (Torrington, 1690): a fleet that commands the sea by existing, without giving battle]</span> is posed, that is to say the task of occupying an open space, with a *tourbillonnaire* movement whose effect can *surgir* at any point whatever. In this regard, the recent studies on ***rythme*** <span style="color:green">[rhythm – Benveniste's « La notion de rythme dans son expression linguistique » (1951): Greek rhuthmos first meant form, not the flow of waves]</span>, on the origin of this notion, do not seem to us entirely convincing. For we are told that rhythm has nothing to do with the movement of the ***flots*** <span style="color:green">[waves, billows – the sea's moving water]</span>, but designates « form » in general, and more especially the form of a « measured, cadenced » movement.²⁰ Yet rhythm and measure are never *confondus*. And if the atomist Democritus is precisely one of the authors who employ rhythm in the sense of form, one must not forget that it is in very precise conditions of fluctuation, and that the forms of atoms constitute first of all great non-metric ***ensembles*** <span style="color:green">[sets, wholes – also the mathematician's set; Massumi: "aggregates"]</span>, *lisse* spaces such as the air, the sea, or even the earth (***magnae res*** <span style="color:green">[Latin: great things – Lucretius's large bodies, the elements]</span>). There is indeed a measured, cadenced rhythm, which refers to the *écoulement* of the river between its *rives* or to the form of a *strié* space; but there is also a rhythm without measure, which refers to the ***fluxion*** <span style="color:green">[fluxion – the flowing of a flow; also Newton's fluxion, the rate of change, his name for the derivative; Massumi: "upswell", which loses the calculus]</span> of a *flux*, that is to say to the way a fluid occupies a *lisse* space. This opposition or rather this ***tension-limite*** <span style="color:green">[limit-tension – a tension that exists only as a limit between the two, never resolved]</span> of the two sciences, nomad science of the *machine de guerre* and royal science of the State, is found again at different moments, at different levels. The works of Anne Querrien allow one to *repérer* two of these moments, the one with the construction of Gothic cathedrals in the<br>Notes: ¹⁹ [Minn: 24, Fr: 19] Michel Serres (pp. 105 ff.) analyzes in this regard the opposition d'Alembert-Bernoulli. It is a matter more generally of a difference between two models of space: « The Mediterranean basin lacks water, and the one who holds *pouvoir* is the one who drains the waters. |
| 12¶30 fr.449 umn.364 | D'où ce monde physique où le drain est d'essence, et où le clinamen paraît la liberté parce qu'il est justement cette turbulence qui refuse l'écoulement forcé. Incompréhensible par la théorie scientifique, incompréhensible par le maître des eaux. (...) D'où la grande figure d'Archimède : maître des corps flottants et des machines militaires. » 20. Cf. Benveniste, Problèmes de linguistique générale, « La notion de rythme dans son expression linguistique », pp. 327-375. Ce texte, souvent considéré comme décisif, nous paraît ambigu, parce qu'il invoque Démocrite et l'atomisme sans tenir compte du problème hydraulique, et parce qu'il fait du rythme une « spécialisation secondaire » de la forme corporelle. | [Notes only: Massumi's notes 24-25 are endnotes, outside the body text.] | [Notes only: the page-foot text of fns. 19-20; the body resumes at 12¶31.]<br>Notes: ¹⁹ (cont.) Whence this physical world where the ***drain*** <span style="color:green">[drain – the channel that carries water off; drainage as the hydraulic State's operation]</span> is of the essence, and where the *clinamen* appears as freedom because it is precisely this turbulence that refuses the ***écoulement forcé*** <span style="color:green">[forced flow – flow made to run in a channel, against the swerve]</span>. Incomprehensible by scientific theory, incomprehensible by the ***maître des eaux*** <span style="color:green">[master of the waters – the hydraulic sovereign of Wittfogel's despotism]</span>. (…) Whence the great figure of Archimedes: master of floating bodies and of military machines. » ²⁰ [Minn: 25, Fr: 20] Cf. Benveniste, <u>Problèmes de linguistique générale</u>, « La notion de rythme dans son expression linguistique », pp. 327-375. This text, often considered decisive, seems to us ambiguous, because it invokes Democritus and atomism without taking account of the hydraulic problem, and because it makes rhythm a « secondary specialization » of bodily form. [English: <u>Problems in General Linguistics</u>, trans. Mary Elizabeth Meek, Miami, 1971.] |
| 12¶31 fr.451 umn.364 | XIIe siècle, l'autre avec la construction des ponts aux XVIIIe-XIXe siècles 21. En effet, le gothique est inséparable d'une volonté de construire des églises plus longues et plus hautes que les romanes. Toujours plus loin, toujours plus haut... Mais cette différence n'est pas simplement quantitative, elle marque un changement qualitatif : le rapport statique forme-matière tend à s'estomper au profit d'un rapport dynamique matériau-forces. C'est la taille qui fera de la pierre un matériau capable de saisir et de composer les forces de poussée, et de construire des voûtes toujours plus hautes et plus longues. La voûte n'est plus une forme, mais une ligne de variation continue des pierres. C'est comme si le gothique conquérait un espace lisse, tandis que le roman restait partiellement dans un espace strié (où la voûte dépendait de la juxtaposition de piliers parallèles). Or la taille des pierres est inséparable d'une part d'un plan de projection à même le sol, qui fonctionne comme limite plane, d'autre part d'une série d'approximations successives (équarissage), ou de mises en variation des pierres volumineuses. Certes, on pensa à la science théorématique d'Euclide pour fonder l'entreprise : les chiffres et les équations seraient la forme intelligible capable d'organiser surfaces et volumes. Mais, suivant la légende, Bernard de Clairvaux y renonce vite, comme trop « difficile », et se réclame de la spécificité d'une géométrie opératoire archimédienne, projective et descriptive, définie comme science mineure, mathégraphie plus que mathélogie. | in the twelfth century, the other the construction of bridges in the eighteenth and nineteenth centuries.²⁶ Gothic architecture is indeed inseparable from a will to build churches longer and taller than the Romanesque churches. Ever farther, ever higher... But this difference is not simply quantitative; it marks a qualitative change: the static relation, form-matter, tends to fade into the background in favor of a dynamic relation, material-forces. It is the cutting of the stone that turns it into material capable of holding and coordinating forces of thrust, and of constructing ever higher and longer vaults. The vault is no longer a form but the line of continuous variation of the stones. It is as if Gothic conquered a smooth space, while Romanesque remained partially within a striated space (in which the vault depends on the juxtaposition of parallel pillars). But stone cutting is inseparable from, on the one hand, a plane of projection at ground level, which functions as a plane limit, and, on the other hand, a series of successive approximations (squaring), or placings-in-variation of voluminous stones. Of course, one appealed to the theorematic science of Euclid in order to find a foundation for the enterprise: mathematical figures and equations were thought to be the intelligible form capable of organizing surfaces and volumes. But according to the legend, Bernard de Clairvaux quickly abandoned the effort as too "difficult," appealing to the specificity of an operative, Archimedean geometry, a projective and descriptive geometry defined as a minor science, more a mathegraphy than a matheology. | twelfth century, the other with the construction of bridges in the eighteenth-nineteenth centuries.²¹ In effect, the Gothic is inseparable from a will to construct churches longer and higher than the Romanesque ones. Always further, always higher… But this difference is not simply quantitative, it marks a qualitative change: the static relation ***forme-matière*** <span style="color:green">[form-matter – Aristotle's hylomorphism, form imposed on passive matter, which Simondon's L'individu et sa genèse physico-biologique (1964) contests]</span> tends to ***s'estomper*** <span style="color:green">[blur, fade – estomper: to shade a drawing with a stump, smudging its lines]</span> to the profit of a dynamic relation ***matériau-forces*** <span style="color:green">[material-forces – matériau: worked material with its own traits, not matière as formless stuff]</span>. It is the ***taille*** <span style="color:green">[cutting – stone-cutting, the stereotomy of the masons; also size and the cut of a garment]</span> that will make of the stone a material capable of ***saisir*** <span style="color:green">[seizing, grasping – capturing forces; Massumi: "holding"]</span> and of ***composer*** <span style="color:green">[composing – combining forces as a musician composes; Massumi: "coordinating"]</span> the forces of ***poussée*** <span style="color:green">[thrust – architecture: the lateral push of a vault on its supports, taken up by buttresses]</span>, and of constructing vaults always higher and longer. The vault is no longer a form, but a line of continuous variation of the stones. It is as if the Gothic conquered a *lisse* space, while the Romanesque remained partially in a *strié* space (where the vault depended on the juxtaposition of parallel pillars). Now, the *taille* of the stones is inseparable, on the one hand, from a plane of projection ***à même le sol*** <span style="color:green">[right on the ground – drawn full-scale on the floor of the lodge, the masons' épure]</span>, which functions as a plane limit, on the other hand, from a series of successive approximations (***équarrissage*** <span style="color:green">[squaring – dressing a stone to right angles; also the flaying and rendering of carcasses]</span>), or of ***mises en variation*** <span style="color:green">[placings-in-variation – putting into variation, as a musical theme is varied]</span> of the voluminous stones. Certainly, one thought of the theorematic science of Euclid to found the enterprise: ***chiffres*** <span style="color:green">[figures, numbers – digits, ciphers; Massumi: "mathematical figures"]</span> and equations would be the intelligible form capable of organizing surfaces and volumes. But, according to the legend, Bernard of Clairvaux renounces it quickly, as too « difficult », and *se réclame* of the specificity of an operative Archimedean geometry, projective and descriptive, defined as minor science, ***mathégraphie plus que mathélogie*** <span style="color:green">[mathegraphy more than mathelogy – mathematics as drawing (graphein) rather than as discourse (logos); Massumi: "matheology", which turns logos into theos]</span>. |
| 12¶32 fr.451 umn.364-65 | Son compagnon, le moine-maçon Garin de Troyes, invoque une logique opératoire du mouvement qui permet à l'« initié » de tracer, puis de couper les volumes en pénétration dans l'espace, et de faire que « le trait pousse le chiffre 22 ». On ne représente pas, on engendre et on parcourt. C'est moins l'absence d'équations qui caractérise cette science que le rôle très différent qu'elles ont éventuellement : au lieu d'être de bonnes formes absolument qui organisent la matière, elles sont « générées », comme « poussées » par le matériau, dans un calcul qualitatif d'optimum. Toute cette géométrie archimédienne aura sa plus haute expression, mais aussi rencontrera son coup d'arrêt provisoire, avec l'étonnant mathématicien Desargues, au XVIIe siècle. Comme la plupart de ses semblables, Desargues écrit peu ; il a pourtant une grande influence en acte, et laisse des ébauches, des brouillons, des projets toujours centrés sur des problèmes-événements : « leçon des ténèbres », « brouillon projet de la coupe des pierres », « brouil[...] 21. Anne Querrien, Devenir fonctionnaire ou le travail de l'Etat, Cerfi. Nous nous servons de ce livre, ainsi que d'études inédites d'Anne Querrien. 22. Cf. Raoul Vergez, Les illuminés de l'art royal, Julliard. | His journeyman, the monk-mason Garin de Troyes, speaks of an operative logic of movement enabling the "initiate" to draw, then hew the volumes "in penetration in space," to make it so that "the cutting line propels the equation" (le trait pousse le chiffre).²⁷ One does not represent, one engenders and traverses. This science is characterized less by the absence of equations than by the very different role they play: instead of being good forms absolutely that organize matter, they are "generated" as "forces of thrust" (poussees) by the material, in a qualitative calculus of the optimum. This whole current of Archimedean geometry was taken to its highest expression, but was also brought to a temporary standstill, by the remarkable seventeenth-century mathematician Desargues. Like most of his kind, Desargues wrote little; he nevertheless exerted a great influence through his actions and left outlines, rough drafts, and projects, all centered on problem-events: "Lamentations," "draft project for the cutting of stones," | His ***compagnon*** <span style="color:green">[companion, journeyman – a member of the compagnonnage, the itinerant guilds of masons and carpenters]</span>, the monk-mason Garin de Troyes, invokes an operative logic of movement that permits the « ***initié*** <span style="color:green">[initiate – one admitted to the craft's secrets]</span> » to trace, then to cut, the volumes in penetration in space, and to make « the line pushes the number <span style="color:green">[le trait pousse le chiffre – the drawn line pushes the number: the stroke drives the calculation; Massumi: "the cutting line propels the equation"; trait: line, stroke, the masons' layout drawing; chiffre as at 12¶31]</span> ».²² One does not represent, one ***engendre et on parcourt*** <span style="color:green">[engenders and travels through – parcourir: to run across, to traverse a course (cf. parcours, 12¶21)]</span>. It is less the absence of equations that characterizes this science than the very different role that they eventually have: instead of being absolutely good forms that organize matter, they are « generated », as « *poussées* » by the material, in a qualitative calculus of optimum. This whole Archimedean geometry will have its highest expression, but also will meet its provisional ***coup d'arrêt*** <span style="color:green">[sudden halt – a blow that stops: the checking of a movement by force; Massumi: "temporary standstill"]</span>, with the astonishing mathematician Desargues, in the seventeenth century. Like most of his kind, Desargues writes little; he has nonetheless a great influence ***en acte*** <span style="color:green">[in act, in practice – in actu, against in writing; Massumi: "through his actions"]</span>, and leaves sketches, ***brouillons*** <span style="color:green">[rough drafts – Desargues's own word in his titles: Brouillon project (1639)]</span>, projects always centered on ***problèmes-événements*** <span style="color:green">[problem-events – problems taken as events that happen to figures (12¶24, 12¶25)]</span>: « ***leçon des ténèbres*** <span style="color:green">[lesson of darkness – the Leçons de ténèbres, Holy Week readings from Lamentations; for Desargues also lessons on shadows, the projection of light; Massumi: "Lamentations"]</span> », « rough draft project of the cutting of stones », « rough<br>Notes: ²¹ [Minn: 26, Fr: 21] Anne Querrien, <u>Devenir fonctionnaire ou le travail de l'Etat</u>, Cerfi. We make use of this book, as well as unpublished studies by Anne Querrien. ²² [Minn: 27, Fr: 22] Cf. Raoul Vergez, <u>Les illuminés de l'art royal</u>, Julliard. |
| 12¶33 fr.452 umn.365 | [brouil]lon projet d'une atteinte aux événements des rencontres d'un cône avec un plan »… Or Desargues est condamné par le parlement de Paris, combattu par le secrétaire du roi ; ses pratiques de perspective sont interdites 23. La science royale ou d'Etat ne supporte et ne s'approprie la taille des pierres que par panneaux (le contraire de l'équarissage), dans des conditions qui restaurent le primat du modèle fixe de la forme, du chiffre et de la mesure. La science royale ne supporte et ne s'approprie la perspective que statique, assujettie à un trou noir central qui lui retire toute capacité heuristique et déambulatoire. Mais l'aventure ou l'événement de Desargues, c'est le même qui s'était déjà produit collectivement pour les « compagnons » gothiques. Car non seulement l'Eglise, sous sa forme impériale, avait éprouvé le besoin de contrôler sévèrement le mouvement de cette science nomade : elle confiait aux Templiers le soin d'en fixer les lieux et les objets, de gouverner les chantiers, de discipliner la construction ; mais, plus encore, l'Etat laïc sous sa forme royale se retourne contre les Templiers eux-mêmes, condamne les compagnonnages, pour toutes sortes de motifs, dont l'un au moins concerne l'interdiction de cette géométrie opératoire ou mineure. Anne Querrien a-t-elle raison de trouver encore un écho de la même histoire, au niveau des ponts, au XVIIIe siècle ? Sans doute les conditions sont très différentes, puisque la division du travail est alors acquise suivant les normes d'Etat. Reste que, dans l'ensemble des activités des Ponts et chaussées, les routes sont l'affaire d'une administration bien centralisée, tandis que les ponts sont encore matière à expérimentation active, dynamique et collective. | draft project for grappling with the events of the encounters of a cone and a plane"... Desargues, however, was condemned by the parlement of Paris, opposed by the king's secretary; his practices of perspective were banned.²⁸ Royal, or State, science only tolerates and appropriates stone cutting by means of templates (the opposite of squaring), under conditions that restore the primacy of the fixed model of form, mathematical figures, and measurement. Royal science only tolerates and appropriates perspective if it is static, subjected to a central black hole divesting it of its heuristic and ambulatory capacities. But the adventure, or event, of Desargues is the same one that had already occurred among the Gothic "journeymen" on a collective level. For not only did the Church, in its imperial form, feel the need to strictly control the movement of this nomad science (it entrusted the Templars with the responsibility of determining its locations and objects, governing the work sites, and regulating construction), but the secular State, in its royal form, turned against the Templars themselves, banning the guilds for a number of reasons, at least one of which was the prohibition of this operative or minor geometry. Is Anne Querrien right to find yet another echo of the same story in the case of bridges in the eighteenth century? Doubtless, the conditions were very different, for the division of labor according to State norms was by then an accomplished fact. But the fact remains that in the government agency in charge of bridges and roadways, roadways were under a well-centralized administration while bridges were still the object of active, dynamic, and collective experimentation. | draft project of an ***atteinte*** <span style="color:green">[reaching, attaining – also an attack, an injury; Desargues's title (1639) spells it « atteinte aux événements »; Massumi: "grappling with"]</span> upon the events of the encounters of a cone with a plane »… Now Desargues is condemned by the ***parlement*** <span style="color:green">[parlement – the sovereign court of justice of the Ancien Régime, not a legislature; Massumi keeps the French]</span> of Paris, fought by the secretary of the king; his practices of perspective are forbidden.²³ Royal or State science ***ne supporte*** <span style="color:green">[does not tolerate – supporter: to bear, to put up with]</span> and does not appropriate the *taille* of stones except by ***panneaux*** <span style="color:green">[templates – cut wooden or metal panels traced on the stone, a fixed model; Massumi: "templates"]</span> (the contrary of *équarrissage*), in conditions that restore the primacy of the fixed model of form, of *chiffre*, and of measure. Royal science *ne supporte* and does not appropriate perspective except as static, subjected to a central ***trou noir*** <span style="color:green">[black hole – the vanishing point; the black hole of subjectivity in the faciality plateau (7)]</span> that withdraws from it every ***heuristique et déambulatoire*** <span style="color:green">[heuristic and ambulatory – finding by searching, and walking about: the eye that moves, against the fixed point of view]</span> capacity. But the adventure or the *événement* of Desargues is the same one that had already happened collectively for the Gothic « *compagnons* ». For not only had the Church, under its imperial form, felt the need to control severely the movement of this nomad science: it entrusted the ***Templiers*** <span style="color:green">[Templars – the military-monastic order (1119-1312), bankers and builders, suppressed by Philip IV of France]</span> with the care of fixing its places and objects, governing the ***chantiers*** <span style="color:green">[work sites, building yards – the lodge and its yard]</span>, disciplining construction; but, still more, the ***laïc*** <span style="color:green">[lay, secular]</span> State under its royal form turns against the *Templiers* themselves, condemns the ***compagnonnages*** <span style="color:green">[journeymen's guilds – the brotherhoods of itinerant craftsmen; Massumi: "guilds", which suggests the settled urban corporations]</span>, for all sorts of motives, of which one at least concerns the forbidding of this operative or minor geometry. Is Anne Querrien right to find yet an echo of the same history, at the level of bridges, in the eighteenth century? Doubtless the conditions are very different, since the division of labor is then acquired according to the norms of State. It remains that, in the ensemble of activities of the ***Ponts et chaussées*** <span style="color:green">[Bridges and Roads – the royal engineering corps founded 1716, and its school (1747)]</span>, roads are the affair of a well-centralized administration, while bridges are still matter for active, dynamic, and collective experimentation. |
| 12¶34 fr.452 umn.365-66 | Trudaine organise chez lui de curieuses « assemblées générales » libres. Perronet s'inspire d'un modèle souple venu d'Orient : que le pont ne bouche ou n'obstrue pas la rivière. A la gravité du pont, à l'espace strié des piles épaisses et régulières, il oppose l'amincissement et la discontinuité des piles, le surbaissement de la voûte, la légèreté et la variation continue de l'ensemble. Mais la tentative se heurte vite à des oppositions de principe ; et suivant un procédé fréquent, en nommant Perronet directeur de l'école, l'Etat inhibe l'expérimentation plus qu'il ne la couronne. C'est toute l'histoire de l'Ecole des ponts et chaussées qui montre comment ce « corps », ancien et roturier, va être subordonné aux Mines, aux Travaux publics, à Polytechnique, en même temps que ses activités, de plus en plus normalisées 24. On arrive donc à la question : qu'est-ce qu'un [...] 23. Desargues, Œuvres, Ed. Leiber (avec le texte de Michel Chasles, qui établit une continuité entre Desargues, Monge et Poncelet comme « fondateurs d'une géométrie moderne »). 24. Anne Querrien, pp. 26-27 : « L'Etat se construit-il sur la faillite [...] corps collectif ? Et sans doute les grands corps d'un Etat sont des organismes différenciés et hiérarchisés qui, d'une part, disposent du monopole d'un pouvoir ou d'une fonction, d'autre part répartissent localement leurs représentants. Ils ont un rapport spécial avec les familles, parce qu'ils font communiquer aux deux bouts le modèle familial et le modèle étatique, et se vivent eux-mêmes comme de « grandes familles » de fonctionnaires, de commis, d'intendants ou de fermiers. Toutefois, il semble que, dans beaucoup de ces corps, quelque chose d'autre agit qui ne se ramène pas à ce schéma. Ce n'est pas seulement la défense obstinée de leurs privilèges. | Trudaine organized unusual, open "general assemblies" in his home. Perronet took as his inspiration a supple model originating in the Orient: The bridge should not choke or obstruct the river. To the heaviness of the bridge, to the striated space of thick and regular piles, he opposed a thinning and discontinuity of the piles, surbase, and vault, a lightness and continuous variation of the whole. But his attempt soon ran up against principled opposition; the State, in naming Perronet director of the school, followed a frequently used procedure that inhibited experimentation more than crowning its achievements. The whole history of the Ecole des Ponts et Chaussees (School of Bridges and Roadways) illustrates how this old, plebeian "corps" was subordinated to the Ecole des Mines, the Ecole des Travaux Publics, and the Ecole Polytechnique, at the same time as its activities were increasingly normalized.²⁹ We thus come to the question, What is a collective body? Undoubtedly, the great collective bodies of a State are differentiated and hierarchical organisms that on the one hand enjoy a monopoly over a power or function and on the other hand send out local representatives. They have a special relation to families, because they link the family model to the State model at both ends and regard themselves as "great families" of functionaries, clerks, intendants, or farmers. Yet it seems that in many of these collective bodies there is something else at work that does not fit into this schema. It is not just their obstinate defense of their privileges. | Trudaine organizes at his home curious free « ***assemblées générales*** <span style="color:green">[general assemblies – the deliberating body of all members, as in a guild or a revolutionary section]</span> ». Perronet is inspired by a ***souple*** <span style="color:green">[supple, flexible]</span> model come from the Orient: that the bridge not stop up or obstruct the river. To the ***gravité*** <span style="color:green">[heaviness, weight – also gravity and gravitas, seriousness]</span> of the bridge, to the *strié* space of thick and regular ***piles*** <span style="color:green">[piers – the masonry supports of a bridge standing in the river]</span>, he opposes the thinning and discontinuity of the *piles*, the ***surbaissement*** <span style="color:green">[lowering, flattening – a surbased arch, rising less than half its span; Massumi: "surbase", which makes it a third item in a list]</span> of the vault, the lightness and continuous variation of the whole. But the attempt soon collides with oppositions of principle; and following a frequent procedure, by naming Perronet director of the school, the State inhibits the experimentation more than it crowns it. It is the whole history of the École des ponts et chaussées that shows how this « ***corps*** <span style="color:green">[corps, body – the State engineering corps; also a body in every sense, the word D&G turn on in the next sentences]</span> », old and ***roturier*** <span style="color:green">[commoner, plebeian – of non-noble birth]</span>, will be subordinated to the Mines, to Public Works, to Polytechnique, at the same time as its activities, more and more ***normalisées*** <span style="color:green">[normalized, standardized – made to conform to norms]</span>.²⁴ We arrive then at the question: what is a collective *corps*? And doubtless the ***grands corps*** <span style="color:green">[great bodies – the grands corps de l'État, the elite administrative and technical corps (Mines, Ponts, the Inspection des finances)]</span> of a State are differentiated and hierarchized organisms that, on the one hand, dispose of the monopoly of a *pouvoir* or a function, on the other hand distribute their representatives locally. They have a special relation with families, because they make the family model and the State model communicate at the two ends, and they experience themselves as « great families » of functionaries, of clerks, of intendants or of ***fermiers*** <span style="color:green">[tax farmers – the fermiers généraux who leased the right to collect taxes; Massumi: "farmers", which loses the tax]</span>. However, it seems that in many of these *corps* something else acts that does not reduce to this schema. It is not only the obstinate defense of their privileges.<br>Notes: ²³ [Minn: 28, Fr: 23] Desargues, <u>Œuvres</u>, Éd. Leiber (with the text of Michel Chasles, who establishes a continuity between Desargues, Monge, and Poncelet as « founders of a modern geometry »). ²⁴ [Minn: 29, Fr: 24] Anne Querrien, pp. 26-27: « Does the State build itself on the failure […] (continues). |
| 12¶35 fr.452 umn.366 | Ce serait aussi une aptitude, même caricaturale, même très déformée, à se constituer comme machine de guerre, en opposant à l'Etat d'autres modèles, un autre dynamisme, une ambition nomade. Il y a par exemple un problème très ancien du lobby, groupe aux contours fluents, de situation très ambiguë, par rapport à l'Etat qu'il veut « influer », et à une machine de guerre qu'il veut promouvoir, quels qu'en soient les buts 25. Un corps ne se réduit pas à un organisme, pas plus que l'esprit de corps ne se réduit à l'âme d'un organisme. L'esprit n'est pas meilleur, mais il est volatile, tandis que l'âme est gravifique, centre de gravité. Faut-il invoquer une origine militaire du corps et de l'esprit de corps ? Ce n'est pas « militaire » qui compte, mais plutôt une origine nomade lointaine. Ibn Khaldoun définissait la machine de guerre nomade par : les familles ou lignages, plus l'esprit de corps. La machine de guerre entretient avec les familles un rapport très différent de celui de l'Etat. Au lieu d'être cellule de base, la famille y est vecteur de bande, si bien qu'une généalogie se transporte d'une famille à une autre, suivant l'aptitude de telle famille, à tel moment, de réaliser le maximum de « solidarité agnatique ». Ce n'est pas l'illustration publique de la famille qui détermine sa place dans un organisme d'Etat, c'est le contraire, c'est la puissance ou vertu secrète de solidarité, et la mouvance correspondante des généalogies, qui déterminent les illustrations dans un corps de guerre 26. Il y a là [suite au 12¶36] [24, suite du 12¶34 : ... la faillite] de l'expérimentation ? (...) L'Etat n'est pas en chantier, ses chantiers doivent être courts. | It is also their aptitude - even caricatural or seriously deformed - to constitute themselves as a war machine, following other models, another dynamism, a nomadic ambition, over against the State. As an example, there is the very old problem of the lobby, a group with fluid contours, whose position is very ambiguous in relation to the State it wishes to "influence" and the war machine it wishes to promote, to whatever ends.³⁰ A body (corps) is not reducible to an organism, any more than esprit de corps is reducible to the soul of an organism. Spirit is not better, but it is volatile, whereas the soul is weighted, a center of gravity. Must we invoke a military origin of the collective body and esprit de corps? "Military" is not the part that counts, but rather the distant nomadic origin. Ibn Khaldun defines the nomad war machine by: families or lineages PLUS esprit de corps. The war machine entertains a relation to families that is very different from its relation to the State. In the war machine, the family is a band vector instead of a fundamental cell; a genealogy is transferred from one family to another according to the aptitude of a given family at a given time to realize the maximum of "agnatic solidarity." Here, it is not the public eminence of a family that determines its place in a State organism but the reverse; it is the secret power (puissance), or strength of solidarity, and the corresponding genealogical mobility that determine its eminence in a war body.³¹ | It ***serait*** <span style="color:green">[would be – conditional again; Massumi: "It is also"]</span> also an aptitude, even caricatural, even very deformed, to constitute itself as *machine de guerre*, by opposing to the State other models, another dynamism, a nomad ambition. There is, for example, a very old problem of the ***lobby*** <span style="color:green">[English in the French – the antechamber where petitioners waited for legislators, then the pressure group]</span>, a group with ***contours fluents*** <span style="color:green">[flowing contours – fluent: in flux, running; Massumi: "fluid contours"]</span>, of very ambiguous situation, in relation to the State that it wants to « ***influer*** <span style="color:green">[influence – influer, literally to flow into; D&G use it transitively, where French wants influer sur]</span> », and to a *machine de guerre* that it wants to promote, whatever its aims may be.²⁵ A *corps* does not reduce itself to an ***organisme*** <span style="color:green">[organism – the organized body, organs subordinated to a whole; cf. the body without organs, against the organism (plateau 6)]</span>, any more than the ***esprit de corps*** <span style="color:green">[esprit de corps – group spirit; here the French rendering of Ibn Khaldun's ʿaṣabiyya, the solidarity of a band]</span> reduces itself to the ***âme*** <span style="color:green">[soul – the anima that animates and unifies an organism]</span> of an *organisme*. Spirit is not better, but it is ***volatile*** <span style="color:green">[volatile – chemistry: evaporating quickly, flying off; Latin volare, to fly]</span>, whereas the soul is ***gravifique*** <span style="color:green">[gravific – a rare coinage: weight-making, giving gravity; Massumi: "weighted"]</span>, center of gravity. Must one invoke a military origin of the *corps* and of the *esprit de corps*? It is not « military » that counts, but rather a distant nomad origin. ***Ibn Khaldoun*** <span style="color:green">[Ibn Khaldun – the Muqaddimah (1377), on the cycle of desert bands and sedentary dynasties]</span> defined the nomad *machine de guerre* by: families or lineages, plus *esprit de corps*. The *machine de guerre* maintains with families a relation very different from that of the State. Instead of being basic cell, the family is there a ***vecteur de bande*** <span style="color:green">[band vector – a carrier of the band, as a vector carries a force in a direction]</span>, so that a genealogy transports itself from one family to another, according to the aptitude of such a family, at such a moment, to realize the maximum of « ***solidarité agnatique*** <span style="color:green">[agnatic solidarity – solidarity among kin related through the male line; Ibn Khaldun's ʿaṣabiyya]</span> ». It is not the public ***illustration*** <span style="color:green">[illustriousness, renown – illustrer: to make illustrious; Massumi: "eminence"]</span> of the family that determines its place in a State *organisme*, it is the contrary, it is the *puissance* or secret ***vertu*** <span style="color:green">[virtue – virtus, power, efficacy, as the virtue of a remedy]</span> of solidarity, and the corresponding ***mouvance*** <span style="color:green">[mobility, shifting – also the feudal mouvance, the dependence of a fief and its sphere of allegiance]</span> of genealogies, that determine the *illustrations* in a ***corps de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[war body – a corps of war, not an army organism]</span>.²⁶ There is here [– the sentence continues at 12¶36]<br>Notes: ²⁴ (cont. from 12¶34) [Does the State build itself on the failure] of experimentation? (…) The State is not under construction, its *chantiers* must be short. |
| 12¶36 fr.452 umn.366-67 | Un équipement est fait pour fonctionner, non pour être construit socialement : de ce point de vue, l'Etat n'appelle à la construction que ceux qui sont payés pour exécuter ou pour ordonner, et qui sont obligés de suivre le modèle d'une expérimentation préétablie. » 25. Sur la question d'un « lobby Colbert », cf. Dessert et Journet, Annales, nov. 1975. 26. Cf. Ibn Khaldoun, La Muqaddima, Hachette. Un des thèmes essentiels de ce chef-d'œuvre, c'est le problème sociologique de l'« esprit de corps », et son ambiguïté. Ibn Khaldoun oppose la bédouinité (comme mode de vie, non pas comme ethnie), et la sédentarité ou citadinité. Parmi [...] quelque chose qui ne se ramène ni au monopole d'un pouvoir organique ni à une représentation locale, mais qui renvoie à la puissance d'un corps tourbillonnaire dans un espace nomade. Et certes, il est difficile de considérer les grands corps d'un Etat moderne comme des tribus arabes. Nous voulons dire plutôt que les corps collectifs ont toujours des franges ou des minorités qui reconstituent des équivalents de machine de guerre, sous des formes parfois très inattendues, dans des agencements déterminés tels que construire des ponts, construire des cathédrales, ou bien rendre des jugements, ou bien faire de la musique, instaurer une science, une technique… Un corps des capitaines fait valoir ses exigences à travers l'organisation des officiers et l'organisme des officiers supérieurs. Viennent toujours des périodes où l'Etat comme organisme a des embarras avec ses propres corps, et où ceux-ci, tout en réclamant des privilèges, sont forcés de s'ouvrir malgré eux sur quelque chose qui les déborde, un court instant révolutionnaire, un élan expérimentateur. | This has to do neither with the monopoly of an organic power (pouvoir) nor with local representation, but is related to the potential (puissance) of a vortical body in a nomad space. Of course, the great bodies of a modern State can hardly be thought of as Arab tribes. What we wish to say, rather, is that collective bodies always have fringes or minorities that reconstitute equivalents of the war machine - in sometimes quite unforeseen forms - in specific assemblages such as building bridges or cathedrals or rendering judgments or making music or instituting a science, a technology... A collective body of captains asserts its demands through the organization of the officers and the organism of the superior officers. There are always periods when the State as organism has problems with its own collective bodies, when these bodies, claiming certain privileges, are forced in spite of themselves to open onto something that exceeds them, a short revolutionary instant, an experimental surge. | [There is here] something that reduces neither to the monopoly of an organic *pouvoir* nor to a local representation, but that refers to the *puissance* of a *tourbillonnaire* *corps* in a nomad space. And certainly, it is difficult to consider the *grands corps* of a modern State as Arab tribes. We mean rather that collective *corps* always have ***franges*** <span style="color:green">[fringes – the frayed edge of a fabric; the fringe of a group]</span> or minorities that reconstitute equivalents of *machine de guerre*, under forms sometimes very unexpected, in determinate *agencements* such as building bridges, building cathedrals, or else ***rendre des jugements*** <span style="color:green">[rendering judgments – the work of judges, the corps of magistrates]</span>, or else making music, instituting a science, a *technique*… A *corps* of captains ***fait valoir*** <span style="color:green">[asserts, puts forward – makes its claims count]</span> its exigencies through the organization of the officers and the *organisme* of the superior officers. There always come periods when the State as *organisme* has ***embarras*** <span style="color:green">[difficulties, troubles – embarrassments, obstructions; Massumi: "problems"]</span> with its own *corps*, and when these, while claiming privileges, are forced in spite of themselves to open onto something that ***les déborde*** <span style="color:green">[overflows them – déborder: to run over the banks, as a river; Massumi: "exceeds"]</span>, a short revolutionary instant, an experimenting ***élan*** <span style="color:green">[surge, impetus – the élan of a charge; Bergson's élan vital]</span>.<br>Notes: ²⁴ (cont.) An ***équipement*** <span style="color:green">[equipment, facility – the CERFI's term for public infrastructure, schools, hospitals, roads; first in Recherches 13, « Généalogie du capital 1. Les équipements du pouvoir » (CERFI, Dec. 1973), with Querrien among its authors and Deleuze, Guattari, and Foucault in its conversations; book ed. 1976]</span> is made to function, not to be constructed socially: from this point of view, the State calls to construction only those who are paid to execute or to order, and who are obliged to follow the model of a pre-established experimentation. » ²⁵ [Minn: 30, Fr: 25] On the question of a « Colbert lobby », cf. Dessert and Journet, <u>Annales</u>, Nov. 1975. ²⁶ [Minn: 31, Fr: 26] Cf. Ibn Khaldun, <u>La Muqaddima</u>, Hachette. One of the essential themes of this masterpiece is the sociological problem of « *esprit de corps* », and its ambiguity. Ibn Khaldun opposes ***bédouinité*** <span style="color:green">[bedouinity – the desert way of life (badāwa), against ḥaḍāra, settled urban life]</span> (as mode of life, not as ethnicity), and sedentarity or ***citadinité*** <span style="color:green">[city-dwelling – urbanity as a way of life; Ibn Khaldun's ḥaḍāra]</span>. Among […] (continues). |
| 12¶37 fr.452 umn.367 | Situation embrouillée, où chaque fois il faut analyser des tendances et des pôles, des natures de mouvements. Tout d'un coup, c'est comme si le corps des notaires avançait en Arabes ou en Indiens, et puis se reprenait, se réorganisait : un opéra-comique, dont on ne sait pas ce qui va sortir (il arrive même qu'on crie : « La police avec nous ! »). Husserl parle d'une proto-géométrie qui s'adresserait à des essences morphologiques vagues, c'est-à-dire vagabondes ou nomades. Ces essences se distingueraient des choses sensibles, mais également des essences idéales, royales ou impériales. La science qui en traiterait, la proto-géométrie, serait elle-même vague, au sens de vagabonde : elle ne serait ni inexacte comme les choses sensibles, ni exacte comme les essences idéales, mais anexacte et pourtant rigoureuse (« inexacte par essence et non par hasard »). Le cercle est une essence fixe idéale, organique, [...] tous les aspects de cette opposition, il y a d'abord le rapport inverse du public et du secret : non seulement il y a un secret de la machine de guerre bédouine, par opposition à la publicité du citadin d'Etat, mais dans le premier cas l'« illustration » découle de la solidarité secrète, tandis que, dans l'autre cas, le secret se subordonne aux exigences d'illustration. En second lieu, la bédouinité joue à la fois d'une grande pureté et d'une grande mobilité des lignages et de leur généalogie, tandis que la citadinité fait des lignages très impurs, et en même temps rigides et fixes : la solidarité change de sens, d'un pôle à l'autre. | A confused situation: each time it occurs, it is necessary to analyze tendencies and poles, the nature of the movements. All of a sudden, it is as if the collective body of the notary publics were advancing like Arabs or Indians, then regrouping and reorganizing: a comic opera where you never know what is going to happen next (even the cry "The police are with us!" is sometimes heard). Husserl speaks of a protogeometry that addresses vague, in other words, vagabond or nomadic, morphological essences. These essences are distinct from sensible things, as well as from ideal, royal, or imperial essences. Protogeometry, the science dealing with them, is itself vague, in the etymological sense of "vagabond": it is neither inexact like sensible things nor exact like ideal essences, but anexact yet rigorous ("essentially and not accidentally inexact"). The circle is an organic, ideal, fixed essence, | An ***embrouillée*** <span style="color:green">[tangled, muddled – brouiller again (12¶9): scrambled]</span> situation, where each time one must analyze tendencies and poles, natures of movements. All at once, it is as if the *corps* of ***notaires*** <span style="color:green">[notaries – the French notariat, a closed corps of public officers who draw up deeds and contracts]</span> advanced ***en Arabes ou en Indiens*** <span style="color:green">[as Arabs or as Indians – en: in the manner of, dressed as; the war-whoop of a western]</span>, and then ***se reprenait*** <span style="color:green">[caught itself, took itself back in hand – also recovered, corrected itself]</span>, reorganized itself: an ***opéra-comique*** <span style="color:green">[comic opera – the French genre with spoken dialogue; also a farce]</span>, of which one does not know what will come out (it even happens that one cries: « The police with us! »). Husserl speaks of a ***proto-géométrie*** <span style="color:green">[protogeometry – Husserl's geometry before ideal exactness, Ideas I §74 and the Origin of Geometry]</span> that ***s'adresserait*** <span style="color:green">[would address itself – the conditional of report through the passage, keeping Husserl at a distance; Massumi uses the indicative throughout]</span> to vague ***essences morphologiques*** <span style="color:green">[morphological essences – Husserl's morphologische Wesen, essences of shape grasped in description, against exact essences]</span>, that is to say vagabond or nomad. These essences would distinguish themselves from sensible things, but equally from ideal, royal or imperial essences. The science that would treat them, protogeometry, would itself be ***vague*** <span style="color:green">[vague – Latin vagus, wandering, roving; the root of vagabond]</span>, in the sense of vagabond: it would be neither inexact like sensible things, nor exact like ideal essences, but ***anexacte*** <span style="color:green">[anexact – Husserl's anexakt: neither exact nor inexact, a distinct kind of precision]</span> and yet rigorous (« inexact by essence and not by chance »). The circle is a fixed ideal essence, organic,<br>Notes: ²⁶ (cont.) [Among] all the aspects of this opposition, there is first the inverse relation of the public and the secret: not only is there a secret of the Bedouin *machine de guerre*, as opposed to the ***publicité*** <span style="color:green">[publicity – being public, open to view; not advertising]</span> of the State city-dweller, but in the first case « *illustration* » flows from the secret solidarity, whereas, in the other case, the secret subordinates itself to the exigencies of *illustration*. In the second place, *bédouinité* plays at once on a great purity and a great mobility of lineages and of their genealogy, whereas *citadinité* makes lineages very impure, and at the same time rigid and fixed: solidarity changes sense, from one pole to the other. |
| 12¶38 fr.452 umn.367 | En troisième lieu et surtout, les lignages bédouins mobilisent un « esprit de corps » et s'intègrent en lui comme nouvelle dimension : c'est l'Açabiyya, ou bien l'Ichtirak, d'où dérivera le nom arabe du socialisme (Ibn Khaldoun insiste sur l'absence de « pouvoir » du chef de tribu, qui ne dispose pas de contrainte étatique). Tandis que la citadinité fait de l'esprit de corps une dimension du pouvoir, et va l'approprier à l'« autocratie ». | [Notes only: Massumi's note 31 is an endnote, outside the body text.] | [Notes only: the page-foot text of fn. 26; the body resumes at 12¶39.]<br>Notes: ²⁶ (cont.) In the third place and above all, Bedouin lineages mobilize an « *esprit de corps* » and integrate themselves in it as a new dimension: this is the ***Açabiyya*** <span style="color:green">[Arabic ʿaṣabiyya – group feeling, the solidarity of kin and clients that Ibn Khaldun makes the motor of dynasties]</span>, or else the ***Ichtirak*** <span style="color:green">[Arabic ishtirāk – sharing, partnership; ishtirākiyya became the modern Arabic word for socialism]</span>, from which the Arabic name of socialism will derive (Ibn Khaldun insists on the absence of « *pouvoir* » of the chief of the tribe, who does not dispose of State constraint). Whereas *citadinité* makes of *esprit de corps* a dimension of *pouvoir*, and will appropriate it to « autocracy ». |
| 12¶39 fr.455 umn.367-68 | mais le rond est une essence vague et fluente qui se distingue à la fois du cercle et des choses arrondies (un vase, une roue, le soleil). Une figure théorématique est une essence fixe, mais ses transformations, déformations, ablations ou augmentations, toutes ses variations, forment des figures problématiques vagues et pourtant rigoureuses, en forme de « lentille », d'« ombelle » ou de « salière ». On dirait que les essences vagues dégagent des choses une détermination qui est plus que la choséité, qui est celle de la corporéité, et qui implique peut-être même un esprit de corps 27. Mais pourquoi Husserl y voit-il une protogéométrie, une sorte d'intermédiaire et non pas de science pure ? Pourquoi fait-il dépendre les essences pures d'un passage à la limite, alors que tout passage à la limite appartient comme tel au vague ? Il y a plutôt là deux conceptions de la science, formellement différentes ; et, ontologiquement, un seul et même champ d'interaction où une science royale ne cesse pas de s'approprier les contenus d'une science nomade ou vague, et où une science nomade ne cesse pas de faire fuir les contenus de la science royale. A la limite, seule compte la frontière constamment mobile. Chez Husserl (et aussi chez Kant, bien qu'en sens inverse, le rond comme « schème » du cercle), on constate une très juste appréciation de l'irréductibilité de la science nomade, mais en même temps un souci d'homme d'Etat, ou qui prend parti pour l'Etat, de maintenir un primat législatif et constituant de la science royale. Chaque fois que l'on en reste à ce primat, on fait de la science nomade une instance préscientifique, ou parascientifique, ou subscientifique. Et surtout, on ne peut plus comprendre les rapports science-technique, science-pratique, puisque la science nomade n'est pas une simple technique ou pratique, mais un champ scientifique dans lequel le problème de ces rapports se pose et se résout tout autrement que du point de vue de la science royale. L'Etat ne cesse de produire et reproduire des cercles idéaux, mais il faut une machine de guerre pour faire un rond. C'est donc les caractères propres de la science nomade qu'il faudrait déterminer, pour comprendre à la fois la répression qu'elle subit et l'interaction dans laquelle elle se « tient ». | but roundness is a vague and fluent essence, distinct both from the circle and things that are round (a vase, a wheel, the sun). A theorematic figure is a fixed essence, but its transformations, distortions, ablations, and augmentations, all of its variations, form problematic figures that are vague yet rigorous, "lens-shaped," "umbelliform," or "indented." It could be said that vague essences extract from things a determination that is more than thinghood (choséité), which is that of corporeality (corporéité), and which perhaps even implies an esprit de corps.³² But why does Husserl see this as a protogeometry, a kind of halfway point and not a pure science? Why does he make pure essences dependent upon a passage to the limit, when any passage to the limit belongs as such to the vague? What we have, rather, are two formally different conceptions of science, and, ontologically, a single field of interaction in which royal science continually appropriates the contents of vague or nomad science while nomad science continually cuts the contents of royal science loose. At the limit, all that counts is the constantly shifting borderline. In Husserl (and also in Kant, though in the opposite direction: roundness as the "schema" of the circle), we find a very accurate appreciation of the irreducibility of nomad science, but simultaneously the concern of a man of the State, or one who sides with the State, to maintain a legislative and constituent primacy for royal science. Whenever this primacy is taken for granted, nomad science is portrayed as a prescientific or parascientific or subscientific agency. And most important, it becomes impossible to understand the relations between science and technology, science and practice, because nomad science is not a simple technology or practice, but a scientific field in which the problem of these relations is brought out and resolved in an entirely different way than from the point of view of royal science. The State is perpetually producing and reproducing ideal circles, but a war machine is necessary to make something round. Thus the specific characteristics of nomad science are what need to be determined in order to understand both the repression it encounters and the interaction "containing" it. | [but the round] is a vague and ***fluente*** <span style="color:green">[fluent, flowing – as the contours of 12¶35]</span> essence that distinguishes itself at once from the circle and from rounded things (a vase, a wheel, the [sun]). A theorematic figure is a fixed essence, but its transformations, deformations, ablations or augmentations, all its variations, form problematic figures, vague and yet rigorous, in the form of a « lens », of an « ***ombelle*** <span style="color:green">[umbel – botany: a flower cluster whose stalks spread from one point, like an umbrella]</span> » or of a « ***salière*** <span style="color:green">[salt cellar – the hollowed shape of a salt dish; Massumi: "indented", perhaps restoring Husserl's own example]</span> ». *On dirait* that vague essences ***dégagent des choses*** <span style="color:green">[draw out from things – dégager (12¶6): to free, to extract; Massumi: "extract from things"]</span> a determination that is more than ***choséité*** <span style="color:green">[thinghood – Husserl's Dinglichkeit, what makes a thing a thing]</span>, that is that of ***corporéité*** <span style="color:green">[corporeality – bodiliness, Husserl's Körperlichkeit; the corps returns from 12¶34-35]</span>, and that implies perhaps even an *esprit de corps*.²⁷ But why does Husserl see in it a protogeometry, a sort of intermediary and not a pure science? Why does he make pure essences depend on a passage to the limit, when every passage to the limit belongs as such to the vague? There are rather two conceptions of science here, formally different; and, ontologically, one and the same field of interaction where a royal science does not cease to appropriate the contents of a nomad or vague science, and where a nomad science does not cease to ***faire fuir*** <span style="color:green">[make flee – set running, put to flight: the line of flight (ligne de fuite); Massumi: "cuts … loose"]</span> the contents of royal science. At the limit, only the constantly mobile ***frontière*** <span style="color:green">[frontier – as at 12¶27; Massumi: "borderline"]</span> counts. In Husserl (and also in Kant, although in the inverse sense, the round as « ***schème*** <span style="color:green">[schema – Kant's schema, the rule of the imagination that brings a concept to intuition (Critique of Pure Reason, A137)]</span> » of the circle), one notes a very just appreciation of the irreducibility of nomad science, but at the same time a *souci* of an *homme d'Etat*, or of one who takes the side of the State, to maintain a ***primat législatif et constituant*** <span style="color:green">[legislative and constituent primacy – the power to lay down law and to constitute objects, Kant's legislating understanding]</span> of royal science. Each time that one stays with this primacy, one makes of nomad science a prescientific, or parascientific, or subscientific ***instance*** <span style="color:green">[instance, agency – an authority or court in a hierarchy (court of first instance); also Freud's Instanz]</span>. And above all, one can no longer understand the relations science-*technique*, science-practice, since nomad science is not a simple *technique* or practice, but a scientific field in which the problem of these relations is posed and resolved altogether otherwise than from the point of view of royal science. The State does not cease to produce and reproduce ideal circles, but one needs a *machine de guerre* to ***faire un rond*** <span style="color:green">[make a round – to make something round, by hand or turning; also faire des ronds, to make circles in water]</span>. It is therefore the proper characters of nomad science that one would have to determine, to understand at once the repression it undergoes and the interaction in which it ***se « tient »*** <span style="color:green">[holds itself, keeps itself – tenir again; Massumi: "the interaction 'containing' it", which makes the interaction the holder]</span>. |
| 12¶40 fr.455 umn.368 | 27. Les textes principaux de Husserl sont Idées I, § 74, Gallimard, et L'origine de la géométrie, P. U. F. (avec le commentaire très important de Derrida, pp. 125-138). Le problème étant celui d'une science vague et pourtant rigoureuse, on se reportera à la formule de Michel Serres, commentant la figure dite Salinon : « Elle est rigoureuse, anexacte. Et non précise, exacte ou inexacte. Seule une métrique est exacte » (Naissance de la physique, p. 29). Le livre de Bachelard, Essai sur la connaissance approchée (Vrin), reste la meilleure étude des démarches et procédés qui constituent toute une rigueur de l'anexact, et de leur rôle créatif dans la science. | [Notes only: Massumi's note 32 is an endnote, outside the body text.] | [Notes only: the page-foot text of fn. 27; the body resumes at 12¶41.]<br>Notes: ²⁷ [Minn: 32, Fr: 27] The principal texts of Husserl are <u>Idées I</u>, § 74, Gallimard, and <u>L'origine de la géométrie</u>, P.U.F. (with the very important commentary of Derrida, pp. 125-138). The problem being that of a vague and yet rigorous science, one will refer to the formula of Michel Serres, commenting on the figure called ***Salinon*** <span style="color:green">[salinon – Greek: salt-cellar; Archimedes' figure of four semicircles in the Book of Lemmas, the source of the « salière » of 12¶39]</span>: « It is rigorous, anexact. And not precise, exact or inexact. Only a metric is exact » (<u>Naissance de la physique</u>, p. 29). The book of Bachelard, <u>Essai sur la connaissance approchée</u> (Vrin), remains the best study of the ***démarches*** <span style="color:green">[approaches, steps – the moves of an inquiry as it proceeds]</span> and procedures that constitute a whole rigor of the anexact, and of their creative role in science. [English: Husserl, <u>Ideas</u> I, § 74; <u>Edmund Husserl's Origin of Geometry: An Introduction</u>, trans. John P. Leavey, 1978.] |
| 12¶41 fr.456 umn.368 | La science nomade n'a pas avec le travail le même rapport que la science royale. Non pas que la division du travail y soit moindre, mais elle est autre. On sait les problèmes que les Etats ont toujours eu avec les « compagnonnages », les corps nomades ou itinérants du type maçons, charpentiers, forgerons, etc. Fixer, sédentariser la force de travail, régler le mouvement du flux de travail, lui assigner des canaux et conduits, faire des corporations au sens d'organismes, et, pour le reste, faire appel à une main-d'œuvre forcée, recrutée sur les lieux (corvée) ou chez les indigents (ateliers de charité), – ce fut toujours une des affaires principales de l'Etat, qui se proposait à la fois de vaincre un vagabondage de bande et un nomadisme de corps. Si nous revenons à l'exemple gothique, c'est pour rappeler combien les compagnons voyageaient, faisant des cathédrales ici et là, essaimant les chantiers, disposant d'une puissance active et passive (mobilité et grève) qui ne convenait certes pas aux Etats. La riposte de l'Etat, c'est gérer les chantiers, faire passer dans toutes les divisions du travail la distinction suprême de l'intellectuel et du manuel, du théorique et du pratique, copiée sur la différence « gouvernants-gouvernés ». Dans les sciences nomades autant que dans les sciences royales, on trouvera l'existence d'un « plan », mais ce n'est pas du tout de la même façon. Au plan à même le sol du compagnon gothique s'oppose le plan métrique sur papier de l'architecte hors chantier. Au plan de consistance ou de composition s'oppose un autre plan, qui est d'organisation et de formation. A la taille des pierres par équarissage s'oppose la taille par panneaux, qui implique l'érection d'un modèle à reproduire. | Nomad science does not have the same relation to work as royal science. Not that the division of labor in nomad science is any less thorough; it is different. We know of the problems States have always had with journeymen's associations, or compagnonnages, the nomadic or itinerant bodies of the type formed by masons, carpenters, smiths, etc. Settling, sedentarizing labor power, regulating the movement of the flow of labor, assigning it channels and conduits, forming corporations in the sense of organisms, and, for the rest, relying on forced manpower recruited on the spot (corvée) or among indigents (charity workshops) - this has always been one of the principal affairs of the State, which undertook to conquer both a band vagabondage and a body nomadism. Let us return to the example of Gothic architecture for a reminder of how extensively the journeymen traveled, building cathedrals near and far, scattering construction sites across the land, drawing on an active and passive power (mobility and the strike) that was far from convenient for the State. The State's response was to take over management of the construction sites, merging all the divisions of labor in the supreme distinction between the intellectual and the manual, the theoretical and the practical, modeled upon the difference between "governors" and "governed." In the nomad sciences, as in the royal sciences, we find the existence of a "plane," but not at all in the same way. The ground-level plane of the Gothic journeyman is opposed to the metric plane of the architect, which is on paper and off site. The plane of consistency or composition is opposed to another plane, that of organization or formation. Stone cutting by squaring is opposed to stone cutting using templates, which implies the erection of a model for reproduction. | Nomad science does not have the same relation with ***travail*** <span style="color:green">[work, labor – the labor-work of plateau 13 (Proposition XIII), here already the State's concern]</span> as royal science. Not that the division of labor is lesser there, but it is other. One knows the problems that States have always had with the « *compagnonnages* », the nomad or itinerant *corps* of the type masons, carpenters, smiths, etc. To fix, to sedentarize labor-force, to regulate the movement of the *flux* of labor, to assign it channels and conduits, to make ***corporations*** <span style="color:green">[guilds, corporations – the chartered trades of the Ancien Régime, a corps made into an organism]</span> in the sense of *organismes*, and, for the rest, to call on a forced ***main-d'œuvre*** <span style="color:green">[labor, workforce – literally hand-work]</span>, recruited on the spot (***corvée*** <span style="color:green">[corvée – unpaid labor owed to a lord or the State, as on roads]</span>) or among the indigent (***ateliers de charité*** <span style="color:green">[charity workshops – public works for the poor under the Ancien Régime, labor in exchange for relief]</span>), – this was always one of the principal affairs of the State, which proposed to itself at once to vanquish a ***vagabondage de bande*** <span style="color:green">[band vagabondage – wandering in bands, the band of 12¶14-16]</span> and a ***nomadisme de corps*** <span style="color:green">[corps nomadism – the nomadism of a corps such as the compagnonnages]</span>. If we come back to the Gothic example, it is to recall how much the *compagnons* traveled, making cathedrals here and there, *essaimant* the *chantiers*, disposing of an active and passive *puissance* (mobility and ***grève*** <span style="color:green">[strike – the work stoppage; from the place de Grève in Paris where the unemployed gathered]</span>) that certainly did not suit States. The ***riposte*** <span style="color:green">[riposte – a counterthrust in fencing; Massumi: "response"]</span> of the State is to manage the *chantiers*, to make pass into all the divisions of labor the supreme distinction of intellectual and manual, of theoretical and practical, copied on the difference « governors-governed ». In the nomad sciences as much as in the royal sciences, one will find the existence of a « ***plan*** <span style="color:green">[plan, plane – one French word for both: the architect's plan and the geometrical plane; the plan de consistance plays on both; a third sense resonates, the French national Plan (Commissariat général du Plan), analyzed in Recherches 13, ch. V, « le Discours du Plan », pp. 195-200 – a resonance, not D&G's stated reference]</span> », but it is not at all in the same way. To the *plan* *à même le sol* of the Gothic *compagnon* is opposed the metric *plan* on paper of the architect ***hors chantier*** <span style="color:green">[off site, away from the work site – the drafting office against the yard]</span>. To the ***plan de consistance ou de composition*** <span style="color:green">[plane of consistency or composition – ATP's plane where heterogeneous elements hold together without a form (plateau 10), against the plane of organization]</span> is opposed another *plan*, which is of organization and of formation. To the *taille* of stones by *équarrissage* is opposed the *taille* by *panneaux*, which implies the erection of a model to reproduce. |
| 12¶42 fr.456 umn.368-69 | On ne dira pas seulement qu'il n'y a plus besoin d'un travail qualifié : il y a nécessité d'un travail non qualifié, d'une déqualification du travail. L'Etat ne confère pas un pouvoir aux intellectuels ou concepteurs, il en fait au contraire un organe étroitement dépendant, qui n'a d'autonomie qu'en rêve, mais qui suffit pourtant à retirer toute puissance à ceux qui ne font plus que reproduire ou exécuter. Ce qui n'empêche pas que l'Etat rencontre encore des difficultés, avec ce corps d'intellectuels qu'il a lui-même engendré, mais qui fait valoir de nouvelles prétentions nomadiques et politiques. En tout cas, si l'Etat est amené perpétuellement à réprimer les sciences mineures et nomades, s'il s'oppose aux essences vagues, à la géométrie opératoire du trait, ce n'est pas en vertu d'un contenu inexact ou imparfait de ces sciences, ni de leur caractère magique ou initiatique, mais parce qu'elles impliquent une division du travail qui s'oppose à celle des normes d'Etat. La différence n'est pas extrinsèque : la manière dont une science, ou une conception de la science participe à l'organisation du champ social, et en particulier induit une division du travail, fait partie de cette science même. La science royale n'est pas séparable d'un modèle « hylémorphique », qui implique à la fois une forme organisatrice pour la matière, et une matière préparée pour la forme ; on a souvent montré comment ce schéma dérivait moins de la technique ou de la vie que d'une société divisée en gouvernants-gouvernés, puis intellectuels-manuels. Ce qui le caractérise, c'est que toute la matière est mise du côté du contenu, tandis que toute la forme passe dans l'expression. | It can be said not only that there is no longer a need for skilled or qualified labor, but also that there is a need for unskilled or unqualified labor, for a dequalification of labor. The State does not give power (pouvoir) to the intellectuals or conceptual innovators; on the contrary, it makes them a strictly dependent organ with an autonomy that is only imagined yet is sufficient to divest those whose job it becomes simply to reproduce or implement of all of their power (puissance). This does not shield the State from more trouble, this time with the body of intellectuals it itself engendered, but which asserts new nomadic and political claims. In any case, if the State always finds it necessary to repress the nomad and minor sciences, if it opposes vague essences and the operative geometry of the trait, it does so not because the content of these sciences is inexact or imperfect, or because of their magic or initiatory character, but because they imply a division of labor opposed to the norms of the State. The difference is not extrinsic: the way in which a science, or a conception of science, participates in the organization of the social field, and in particular induces a division of labor, is part of that science itself. Royal science is inseparable from a "hylomorphic" model implying both a form that organizes matter and a matter prepared for the form; it has often been shown that this schema derives less from technology or life than from a society divided into governors and governed, and later, intellectuals and manual laborers. What characterizes it is that all matter is assigned to content, while all form passes into expression. | One will not say only that there is no longer need of a qualified labor: there is necessity of an unqualified labor, of a ***déqualification*** <span style="color:green">[deskilling – stripping labor of its qualification, the trade's know-how]</span> of labor. The State does not confer a *pouvoir* on intellectuals or ***concepteurs*** <span style="color:green">[designers, conceivers – those who draw up the plan; Massumi: "conceptual innovators"]</span>, it makes of them on the contrary a narrowly dependent organ, which has autonomy only ***en rêve*** <span style="color:green">[in a dream – only in dreams; Massumi: "only imagined"]</span>, but which suffices nonetheless to withdraw all *puissance* from those who do no more than reproduce or execute. Which does not prevent the State from still meeting difficulties, with this *corps* of intellectuals that it has itself engendered, but which *fait valoir* new nomadic and political ***prétentions*** <span style="color:green">[claims – pretensions in the legal sense, what one lays claim to]</span>. In any case, if the State is perpetually led to repress the minor and nomad sciences, if it opposes itself to vague essences, to the operative geometry of the ***trait*** <span style="color:green">[trait, line – the masons' layout drawing, the line that pushes the number (12¶32)]</span>, it is not by virtue of an inexact or imperfect content of these sciences, nor of their magical or initiatory character, but because they imply a division of labor that opposes itself to that of the norms of State. The difference is not extrinsic: the manner in which a science, or a conception of science, participates in the organization of the social field, and in particular induces a division of labor, forms part of that science itself. Royal science is not separable from a « ***hylémorphique*** <span style="color:green">[hylomorphic – from hulē, matter, and morphē, form: Simondon's critique of Aristotle's form imposed on prepared matter]</span> » model, which implies at once an organizing form for matter, and a matter prepared for form; one has often shown how this schema derived less from *technique* or from life than from a society divided into governors-governed, then intellectuals-manuals. What characterizes it is that all matter is put on the side of the ***contenu*** <span style="color:green">[content – Hjelmslev's plane of content, each with its own form and substance (plateau 3)]</span>, whereas all form passes into the ***expression*** <span style="color:green">[expression – Hjelmslev's plane of expression; D&G give each plane both form and matter]</span>. |
| 12¶43 fr.456 umn.369 | Il semble que la science nomade soit plus sensible immédiatement à la connexion du contenu et de l'expression pour eux-mêmes, chacun de ces deux termes ayant forme et matière. C'est ainsi que pour la science nomade la matière n'est jamais une matière préparée, donc homogénéisée, mais est essentiellement porteuse de singularités (qui constituent une forme de contenu). Et l'expression n'est pas davantage formelle, mais inséparable de traits pertinents (qui constituent une matière d'expression). C'est un tout autre schéma, nous le verrons. On a déjà une idée de cette situation si l'on pense au caractère le plus général de l'art nomade, où la connexion dynamique du support et de l'ornement remplace la dialectique matière-forme. Ainsi, du point de vue de cette science qui se présente aussi bien comme art et comme technique, la division du travail existe pleinement, mais n'emprunte pas la dualité forme-matière (même avec des correspondances bi-univoques). Elle suit plutôt les connexions entre des singularités de matière et des traits d'expression, et s'établit au niveau de ces connexions, naturelles ou forcées 28. C'est une autre organisation du travail, et du champ social à travers le travail. Il faudrait opposer deux modèles scientifiques, à la manière de Platon dans le Timée 29. L'un se nommerait Compars, et l'autre Dispars. Le compars est le modèle légal ou légaliste emprunté [...] 28. Gilbert Simondon a poussé très loin l'analyse et la critique du schéma hylémorphique, et de ses présupposés sociaux (« la forme correspond à ce que l'homme qui commande a pensé en lui-même et qu'il doit exprimer de manière positive lorsqu'il donne ses ordres : la forme est donc de l'ordre de l'exprimable »). | It seems that nomad science is more immediately in tune with the connection between content and expression in themselves, each of these two terms encompassing both form and matter. Thus matter, in nomad science, is never prepared and therefore homogenized matter, but is essentially laden with singularities (which constitute a form of content). And neither is expression formal; it is inseparable from pertinent traits (which constitute a matter of expression). This is an entirely different schema, as we shall see. We can get a preliminary idea of this situation by recalling the most general characteristic of nomad art, in which a dynamic connection between support and ornament replaces the matter-form dialectic. From the point of view of nomad science, which presents itself as an art as much as a technique, the division of labor fully exists, but it does not employ the form-matter duality (even in the case of biunivocal correspondences). Rather, it follows the connections between singularities of matter and traits of expression, and lodges on the level of these connections, whether they be natural or forced.³³ This is another organization of work and of the social field through work. It is instructive to contrast two models of science, after the manner of Plato in the Timaeus.³⁴ One could be called Compars and the other Dispars. The compars is the legal or legalist model employed | It seems that nomad science is more immediately ***sensible*** <span style="color:green">[sensitive, responsive – not "sensible" in the English sense; Massumi: "in tune with"]</span> to the connection of content and expression for themselves, each of these two terms having form and matter. It is thus that for nomad science matter is never a prepared matter, therefore homogenized, but is essentially ***porteuse*** <span style="color:green">[bearer, carrier – porteuse de: carrying, as a wave carries a signal; Massumi: "laden with"]</span> of ***singularités*** <span style="color:green">[singularities – mathematics: the remarkable points of a curve or field, where its behavior changes]</span> (which constitute a form of content). And expression is no more formal, but inseparable from ***traits pertinents*** <span style="color:green">[pertinent traits – linguistics: the distinctive features that make a difference in a system (Martinet, Prieto)]</span> (which constitute a matter of expression). It is an altogether other schema, we will see. One already has an idea of this situation if one thinks of the most general character of nomad art, where the dynamic connection of ***support*** <span style="color:green">[support – the carrier, ground, or medium an ornament is set on; in nomad art the bridle, the buckle, the saddle]</span> and ornament replaces the dialectic matter-form. Thus, from the point of view of this science, which presents itself as much as art as *technique*, the division of labor exists fully, but does not ***emprunte*** <span style="color:green">[borrow – emprunter: to borrow, to take a road; Massumi: "employ"]</span> the duality form-matter (even with *bi-univoques* correspondences). It follows rather the connections between singularities of matter and traits of expression, and establishes itself at the level of these connections, natural or forced.²⁸ It is another organization of labor, and of the social field through labor. ***Il faudrait*** <span style="color:green">[one would have to – conditional of necessity; Massumi: "It is instructive to"]</span> oppose two scientific models, in the manner of Plato in the <u>Timaeus</u>.²⁹ The one would be named *Compars*, and the other ***Dispars*** <span style="color:green">[Latin compar, equal, matched, a pair; dispar, unequal, unmatched – Plato's Timaeus sets the model of the same against the model of the other]</span>. The *compars* is the legal or legalist model borrowed<br>Notes: ²⁸ [Minn: 33, Fr: 28] Gilbert Simondon has pushed very far the analysis and critique of the hylomorphic schema, and of its social presuppositions (« form corresponds to what the man who commands has thought in himself and must express in a positive manner when he gives his orders: form is therefore of the order of the expressible »). |
| 12¶44 fr.456 umn.369 | A ce schéma forme-matière, Simondon oppose un schème dynamique, matière pourvue de singularités-forces ou conditions énergétiques d'un système. En sort une tout autre conception des rapports science-technique. Cf. L'individu et sa genèse physico-biologique, P. U. F., pp. 42-56. 29. Dans le texte du Timée (28-29), Platon envisage un court instant que le Devenir ne soit pas seulement le caractère inévitable des copies ou des reproductions, mais soit lui-même un modèle qui rivaliserait avec l'Identique et l'Uniforme.
